Q1. Tens digit \(3\) and digit sum \(4\) are fixed. What is the number?
A. \(30\)
B. \(33\)
C. \(31\)
D. \(32\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Form the digit-sum equation

\[
3+u=4\Rightarrow u=1
\]

Step 2: Write the two-digit number

Hence, the number is 31.

\[
N=10\times 3+1=31
\]

Answer Verification
Digit sum of 31 is 4.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(31\).

Shortcut

Find the ones digit from the digit sum, then place it.

\[
u=1
\]

\[
N=31
\]

Fast result: \(31\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Family: ns_missing_digit_single_rule
---
Q2. Between \(217\) and \(337\), how many integers are divisible by \(18\)?
A. \(7\)
B. \(5\)
C. \(8\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 18 greater than 217 is 234.

\[
18\times 13=234
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 324 is the greatest multiple of 18 not exceeding 337.

\[
337=18\times 18+13
\]

\[
337-13=324
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 18.

\[
\frac{324-234}{18}+1=5+1=6
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 6.
Hence, there are 6 multiples of 18 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(6\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{337}{18}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{216}{18}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
18-12=6
\]

Fast result: \(6\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Family: ns_divisibility_range_count
---
Q3. For the number \(4320\), find the count of positive divisors?
A. \(47\)
B. \(50\)
C. \(48\)
D. \(49\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
4320=2^{5}\times 3^{3}\times 5
\]

Step 2: Apply the (+1) rule on exponents

Each exponent contributes one more choice than its value.

\[
d(4320)=(5+1)\times (3+1)\times (1+1)=48
\]

Answer Verification
The number of positive divisors is 48.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(48\).

Shortcut

Multiply (exponent + 1) across all prime powers.

\[
(5+1)\times (3+1)\times (1+1)
\]

Fast result: \(48\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Family: ns_prime_factorization
---

Shortcut

Fast result: \(6\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q5. Two cyclic events repeat every \(90\) and \(234\) minutes. After how many minutes will they occur together again?
A. \(1287\)
B. \(1404\)
C. \(1053\)
D. \(1170\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[
90=2\times 3^{2}\times 5
\]

\[
234=2\times 3^{2}\times 13
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(90,234)=1170
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 1170.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(1170\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(90,234)=\frac{90\times 234}{\operatorname{HCF}(90,234)}=1170
\]

Fast result: \(1170\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Family: ns_hidden_hcf
---

\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(66,42)=462
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 462.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(462\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(66,42)=\frac{66\times 42}{\operatorname{HCF}(66,42)}=462
\]

Fast result: \(462\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Family: ns_schedule_alignment

Q7. For exponent \(121\), what ending digit does \(23^{121}\) have?
A. \(4\)
B. \(2\)
C. \(5\)
D. \(3\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
23\equiv 3\pmod{10}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 4.

\[
3\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 7\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(121-1)\bmod 4+1=1
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 3.

\[
23^{121}\equiv 3\pmod{10}
\]

Answer Verification
The 1st term of the cycle is 3.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(3\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(121-1)\bmod 4+1=1
\]

\[
3^{121}\equiv 3\pmod{10}
\]

Fast result: \(3\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Family: ns_unit_digit_cycle
---

Q9. With tens \(3\) and ones \(9\), a two-digit number is written. What difference appears after reversing?
A. \(54\)
B. \(55\)
C. \(53\)
D. \(56\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Write both numbers by place value

\[
N_1=10\times 3+9=39
\]

\[
N_2=10\times 9+3=93
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |3-9|=9\times 6=54
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 54.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(54\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |3-9|=54
\]

Fast result: \(54\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Family: ns_digit_interchange

Q10. A number leaves remainder \(5\) when divided by both \(9\) and \(16\). If it is the \(8\)-th such number after zero, what is the number?
A. \(1273\)
B. \(1388\)
C. \(1041\)
D. \(1157\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Translate every clue into a number condition

Reconstruction questions should not be guessed; each clue must become a checkable relation.

\[
N=144\times 8+5
\]

Step 2: Carry the result through the next condition

This makes the final candidate satisfy the full chain, not only the first clue.

\[
N=1157
\]

Answer Verification
The reconstructed value is 1157.
\[
N=1157
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(1157\).

Shortcut

Use the strongest clue first, then verify the candidate against the remaining condition.

\[
Z=1157
\]

Fast result: \(1157\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q11. In \(60!\), find the exponent of \(3\) in its prime factorisation?
A. \(27\)
B. \(30\)
C. \(28\)
D. \(29\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Count powers of 3 in n!

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{60}{3}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{60}{9}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{60}{27}\right\rfloor
\]

Step 2: Add the quotient terms

\[
20+6+2=28
\]

Answer Verification
Highest power of 3 dividing 60! is 28.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(28\).

Shortcut

Legendre sum of floor quotients only.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{60}{3}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{60}{9}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{60}{27}\right\rfloor
\]

Fast result: \(28\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q13. The expression \(11^{42}\) is reduced modulo \(9\). What remainder is obtained?
A. \(0\)
B. \(3\)
C. \(1\)
D. \(2\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
11\equiv 2\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 6.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 7\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(42-1)\bmod 6+1=6
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 1.

\[
11^{42}\equiv 1\pmod{9}
\]

Answer Verification
The 6th term of the cycle is 1.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(42-1)\bmod 6+1=6
\]

\[
2^{42}\equiv 1\pmod{9}
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q14. What least number should be subtracted from \(1386\) so that the result is divisible by \(36\)?
A. \(20\)
B. \(18\)
C. \(19\)
D. \(17\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Combine the number conditions first

Optimization is done only after the divisibility or remainder condition is converted into one usable cycle.

Step 2: Move to the nearest valid boundary

The boundary step prevents picking a number that satisfies only the arithmetic but not the given range.

\[
1386=36\times 38+18
\]

\[
x=18
\]

Answer Verification
The optimized valid value is 18.
\[
N=18
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(18\).

Shortcut

Use the combined cycle and take only the needed boundary correction.

\[
Z=18
\]

Fast result: \(18\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q15. By what least number should \(200\) be multiplied to make it a perfect square?
A. \(4\)
B. \(2\)
C. \(3\)
D. \(1\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Factorize the given number

Perfect powers are controlled by prime exponents.

\[
200=2^{3}\times 5^{2}
\]

Step 2: Complete the exponents

For a square, every exponent must be even.

\[
2^{3}\to 2^{4}
\]

\[
5^{2}\to 5^{2}
\]

Step 3: Read the required value

\[
x=2
\]

Answer Verification
The perfect-power condition is now satisfied.
\[
N=2
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(2\).

Shortcut

Fill only the missing prime powers; do not search through squares or cubes.

\[
M=2
\]

Fast result: \(2\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Family: ns_perfect_square_completion

Q31. When \(7^{57}\) is divided by \(11\), what remainder is left?
A. \(5\)
B. \(8\)
C. \(6\)
D. \(7\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
7\equiv 7\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 10.

\[
7\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 2\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 10\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 6\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(57-1)\bmod 10+1=7
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 6.

\[
7^{57}\equiv 6\pmod{11}
\]

Answer Verification
The 7th term of the cycle is 6.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(6\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(57-1)\bmod 10+1=7
\]

\[
7^{57}\equiv 6\pmod{11}
\]

Fast result: \(6\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\[

\]

Step 2: Apply the (+1) rule on exponents

Each exponent contributes one more choice than its value.

\[
d(108)=(2+1)\times (3+1)=12
\]

Answer Verification
The number of positive divisors is 12.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(12\).

Shortcut

Multiply (exponent + 1) across all prime powers.

\[
(2+1)\times (3+1)
\]

Fast result: \(12\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q41. Between \(211\) and \(366\), how many integers are divisible by \(12\)?
A. \(14\)
B. \(12\)
C. \(15\)
D. \(13\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 12 greater than 211 is 216.

\[
12\times 18=216
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 360 is the greatest multiple of 12 not exceeding 366.

\[
366=12\times 30+6
\]

\[
366-6=360
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 12.

\[
\frac{360-216}{12}+1=12+1=13
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 13.
Hence, there are 13 multiples of 12 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(13\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{366}{12}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{210}{12}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
30-17=13
\]

Fast result: \(13\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q42. Bells ring at intervals of \(45\) and \(54\) minutes. After how many minutes will both ring together again?
A. \(297\)
B. \(256\)
C. \(270\)
D. \(284\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[
45=3^{2}\times 5
\]

\[
54=2\times 3^{3}
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(45,54)=270
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 270.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(270\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(45,54)=\frac{45\times 54}{\operatorname{HCF}(45,54)}=270
\]

Fast result: \(270\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

A. \(17\)
B. \(18\)
C. \(16\)
D. \(19\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 9 greater than 134 is 135.

\[
9\times 15=135
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 279 is the greatest multiple of 9 not exceeding 279.

\[
279=9\times 31+0
\]

\[
279-0=279
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 9.

\[
\frac{279-135}{9}+1=16+1=17
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 17.
Hence, there are 17 multiples of 9 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(17\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{279}{9}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{133}{9}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
31-14=17
\]

Fast result: \(17\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\[

\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 1.

\[
0
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(69-1)\bmod 1+1=1
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 0.

\[

\]

Answer Verification
The 1st term of the cycle is 0.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(0\).

Shortcut

\[
p=(69-1)\bmod 1+1=1
\]

\[
0^{69}\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Fast result: \(0\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\]

Hence, remainder = 1.

\[

\]

\left\lfloor\frac{60}{5}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{60}{25}\right\rfloor
\]

Step 2: Add the quotient terms

\[
12+2=14
\]

Answer Verification
Trailing zeroes in 60! = 14.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(14\).

Shortcut

Sum only ⌊n/5⌋ + ⌊n/25⌋ + …

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{60}{5}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{60}{25}\right\rfloor
\]

Fast result: \(14\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q86. For \(19^{110}\), use the cycle modulo \(17\). What remainder is left?
A. \(13\)
B. \(14\)
C. \(12\)
D. \(15\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
19\equiv 2\pmod{17}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 8.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 16\rightarrow 15\rightarrow 13\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(110-1)\bmod 8+1=6
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 13.

\[
19^{110}\equiv 13\pmod{17}
\]

Answer Verification
The 6th term of the cycle is 13.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(13\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(110-1)\bmod 8+1=6
\]

\[
2^{110}\equiv 13\pmod{17}
\]

Fast result: \(13\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q94. Between \(108\) and \(251\), how many integers are divisible by \(12\)?
A. \(13\)
B. \(11\)
C. \(14\)
D. \(12\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 12 greater than 108 is 108.

\[
12\times 9=108
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 240 is the greatest multiple of 12 not exceeding 251.

\[
251=12\times 20+11
\]

\[
251-11=240
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 12.

\[
\frac{240-108}{12}+1=11+1=12
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 12.
Hence, there are 12 multiples of 12 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(12\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{251}{12}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{107}{12}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
20-8=12
\]

Fast result: \(12\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(72,180)=360
\]

Final Answer

\]

Fast result: \(360\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

A. \(6\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(5\)
D. \(8\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the divisibility rule for 9

A number is divisible by 9 when the sum of its digits is a multiple of 9.

\[
S=12
\]

\[
S+x=12+x
\]

Step 2: Form and solve the digit-sum equation

The completed digit sum must be 18, a multiple of 9.

\[
12+x=18
\]

\[
x=6
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=6\), the digit-sum rule is satisfied.
\[
12+6=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(6\).

Shortcut

Complete the known digit sum to the next multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 6\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=6
\]

Fast result: \(6\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\[

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

\[

\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(31-1)\bmod 20+1=11
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 47.

\[
3^{31}\equiv 47\pmod{100}
\]

Answer Verification
The 11th term of the cycle is 47.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(47\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(31-1)\bmod 20+1=11
\]

\[
3^{31}\equiv 47\pmod{100}
\]

Fast result: \(47\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q117. Between \(123\) and \(265\), how many integers are divisible by \(15\)?
A. \(9\)
B. \(10\)
C. \(8\)
D. \(11\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 15 greater than 123 is 135.

\[
15\times 9=135
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 255 is the greatest multiple of 15 not exceeding 265.

\[
265=15\times 17+10
\]

\[
265-10=255
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 15.

\[
\frac{255-135}{15}+1=8+1=9
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 9.
Hence, there are 9 multiples of 15 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(9\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{265}{15}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{122}{15}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
17-8=9
\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Answer: C

\[

\]

Step 2: Apply the sum-of-divisors rule

Multiply the geometric-sum factor for each prime power.

\[
\sigma(480)=1512
\]

Answer Verification
The sum of all positive divisors is 1512.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(1512\).

Shortcut

Write prime powers once, then multiply the σ-factors.

\[

\]

Fast result: \(1512\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

The LCM is 78.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(78\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(78,78)=\frac{78\times 78}{\operatorname{HCF}(78,78)}=78
\]

Fast result: \(78\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q141. For exponent \(56\), what ending digit does \(7^{56}\) have?
A. \(2\)
B. \(0\)
C. \(3\)
D. \(1\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
7\equiv 7\pmod{10}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 4.

\[
7\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(56-1)\bmod 4+1=4
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 1.

\[
7^{56}\equiv 1\pmod{10}
\]

Answer Verification
The 4th term of the cycle is 1.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(56-1)\bmod 4+1=4
\]

\[
7^{56}\equiv 1\pmod{10}
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q143. Number \(82\) is reversed by interchanging its digits. What absolute difference is obtained?
A. \(56\)
B. \(54\)
C. \(55\)
D. \(53\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Write both numbers by place value

\[
N_1=10\times 8+2=82
\]

\[
N_2=10\times 2+8=28
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |8-2|=9\times 6=54
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 54.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(54\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |8-2|=54
\]

Fast result: \(54\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

\]

\[

\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(38\).

Shortcut

\[

\]

Fast result: \(38\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

D. \(205\)
Answer: A

Perfect powers are controlled by prime exponents.

\[
72=2^{3}\times 3^{2}
\]

Step 2: Complete the exponents

For a cube, every exponent must be a multiple of 3.

\[
2^{3}\to 2^{3}
\]

\[
3^{2}\to 3^{3}
\]

Step 3: Read the required value

\[
72\times 3=216
\]

Answer Verification
The perfect-power condition is now satisfied.
\[
N=216
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(216\).

Shortcut

Fill only the missing prime powers; do not search through squares or cubes.

\[
M=3
\]

Fast result: \(216\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q152. Between \(186\) and \(357\), how many integers are divisible by \(9\)?
A. \(18\)
B. \(21\)
C. \(19\)
D. \(20\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 9 greater than 186 is 189.

\[
9\times 21=189
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 351 is the greatest multiple of 9 not exceeding 357.

\[
357=9\times 39+6
\]

\[
357-6=351
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 9.

\[
\frac{351-189}{9}+1=18+1=19
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 19.
Hence, there are 19 multiples of 9 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(19\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{357}{9}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{185}{9}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
39-20=19
\]

Fast result: \(19\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

C. \(0\)
D. \(3\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
2\equiv 2\pmod{5}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 4.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(28-1)\bmod 4+1=4
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 1.

\[
2^{28}\equiv 1\pmod{5}
\]

Answer Verification
The 4th term of the cycle is 1.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(28-1)\bmod 4+1=4
\]

\[
2^{28}\equiv 1\pmod{5}
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q172. Between \(133\) and \(275\), how many integers are divisible by \(9\)?
A. \(15\)
B. \(18\)
C. \(16\)
D. \(17\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 9 greater than 133 is 135.

\[
9\times 15=135
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 270 is the greatest multiple of 9 not exceeding 275.

\[
275=9\times 30+5
\]

\[
275-5=270
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 9.

\[
\frac{270-135}{9}+1=15+1=16
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 16.
Hence, there are 16 multiples of 9 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(16\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{275}{9}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{132}{9}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
30-14=16
\]

Fast result: \(16\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Fast result: \(27000\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q189. The number \(8x907\) is divisible by \(11\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(3\)
B. \(1\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(2\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the alternating-sum rule for 11

For divisibility by 11, the difference of alternate digit sums must be a multiple of 11.

\[
A=24
\]

\[
24-x\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Solve the alternating-sum equation

The valid multiple of 11 for this pattern is 22.

\[
24-x=22
\]

\[
x=2
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=2\), the alternate digit-sum difference is a multiple of 11.
\[
24-2=22
\]
\[
22\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(2\).

Shortcut

Write the alternate-sum expression and make it a multiple of 11.

\[
x\equiv 2\pmod{11}
\]

\[
x=2
\]

Fast result: \(2\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q192. Power \(3^{96}\) is tested modulo \(13\). Which remainder is obtained?
A. \(1\)
B. \(2\)
C. \(0\)
D. \(3\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
3\equiv 3\pmod{13}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 3.

\[
3\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(96-1)\bmod 3+1=3
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 1.

\[
3^{96}\equiv 1\pmod{13}
\]

Answer Verification
The 3rd term of the cycle is 1.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(96-1)\bmod 3+1=3
\]

\[
3^{96}\equiv 1\pmod{13}
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q195. Number \(49\) is reversed by interchanging its digits. What absolute difference is obtained?
A. \(47\)
B. \(45\)
C. \(46\)
D. \(44\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Write both numbers by place value

\[
N_1=10\times 4+9=49
\]

\[
N_2=10\times 9+4=94
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |4-9|=9\times 5=45
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 45.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(45\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |4-9|=45
\]

Fast result: \(45\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q197. Power \(11^{83}\) is tested modulo \(9\). Which remainder is obtained?
A. \(5\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(7\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
11\equiv 2\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 6.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 7\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(83-1)\bmod 6+1=5
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 5.

\[
11^{83}\equiv 5\pmod{9}
\]

Answer Verification
The 5th term of the cycle is 5.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(83-1)\bmod 6+1=5
\]

\[
2^{83}\equiv 5\pmod{9}
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q204. Between \(175\) and \(337\), how many integers are divisible by \(18\)?
A. \(10\)
B. \(8\)
C. \(11\)
D. \(9\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 18 greater than 175 is 180.

\[
18\times 10=180
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 324 is the greatest multiple of 18 not exceeding 337.

\[
337=18\times 18+13
\]

\[
337-13=324
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 18.

\[
\frac{324-180}{18}+1=8+1=9
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 9.
Hence, there are 9 multiples of 18 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(9\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{337}{18}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{174}{18}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
18-9=9
\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q205. Bells ring at intervals of \(63\) and \(63\) minutes. After how many minutes will both ring together again?
A. \(62\)
B. \(65\)
C. \(63\)
D. \(64\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[
63=3^{2}\times 7
\]

\[
63=3^{2}\times 7
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(63,63)=63
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 63.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(63\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(63,63)=\frac{63\times 63}{\operatorname{HCF}(63,63)}=63
\]

Fast result: \(63\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 6.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 7\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(116-1)\bmod 6+1=2
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 4.

\[
11^{116}\equiv 4\pmod{9}
\]

Answer Verification
The 2nd term of the cycle is 4.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(116-1)\bmod 6+1=2
\]

\[
2^{116}\equiv 4\pmod{9}
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q225. The number \(170x2\) is divisible by \(9\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(8\)
B. \(9\)
C. \(7\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the divisibility rule for 9

A number is divisible by 9 when the sum of its digits is a multiple of 9.

\[
S=10
\]

\[
S+x=10+x
\]

Step 2: Form and solve the digit-sum equation

The completed digit sum must be 18, a multiple of 9.

\[
10+x=18
\]

\[
x=8
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=8\), the digit-sum rule is satisfied.
\[
10+8=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(8\).

Shortcut

Complete the known digit sum to the next multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 8\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=8
\]

Fast result: \(8\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

D. \(1188\)
Answer: D

Explanation:

\[

\]

\[
108=2^{2}\times 3^{3}
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(198,108)=1188
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 1188.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(1188\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(198,108)=\frac{198\times 108}{\operatorname{HCF}(198,108)}=1188
\]

Fast result: \(1188\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q227. For the number \(30\), find the product of its positive divisors?
A. \(1012500\)
B. \(1215000\)
C. \(810000\)
D. \(891000\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
30=2\times 3\times 5
\]

Step 2: Count the divisors

\[
d(30)=(1+1)\times (1+1)\times (1+1)=8
\]

Step 3: Use the product-of-divisors identity

\[
\prod d\mid 30 d=30^{d(30)/2}=30^{8/2}=810000
\]

Answer Verification
The product of all positive divisors is 810000.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(810000\).

Shortcut

After counting divisors, raise n to half the divisor count.

\[
30^{8/2}=810000
\]

Fast result: \(810000\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q237. Between \(227\) and \(396\), how many integers are divisible by \(15\)?
A. \(10\)
B. \(13\)
C. \(11\)
D. \(12\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 15 greater than 227 is 240.

\[
15\times 16=240
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 390 is the greatest multiple of 15 not exceeding 396.

\[
396=15\times 26+6
\]

\[
396-6=390
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 15.

\[
\frac{390-240}{15}+1=10+1=11
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 11.
Hence, there are 11 multiples of 15 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(11\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{396}{15}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{226}{15}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
26-15=11
\]

Fast result: \(11\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\]

\[

\]

Final Answer

\[

\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q248. The number \(685x1\) is divisible by \(11\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(5\)
B. \(3\)
C. \(6\)
D. \(4\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the alternating-sum rule for 11

For divisibility by 11, the difference of alternate digit sums must be a multiple of 11.

\[
A=4
\]

\[
4-x\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Solve the alternating-sum equation

The valid multiple of 11 for this pattern is 0.

\[
4-x=0
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=4\), the alternate digit-sum difference is a multiple of 11.
\[
4-4=0
\]
\[
0\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Write the alternate-sum expression and make it a multiple of 11.

\[
x\equiv 4\pmod{11}
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

\]

\[

\]

Hence, remainder = 3.

\[

\]

Final Answer

\[
p=(54-1)\bmod 10+1=4
\]

\[

Fast result: \(3\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\]

\[

\]

Step 2: Add the quotient terms

\[

Trailing zeroes in 30! = 7.

Final Answer

Shortcut

\[

\]

A. \(16\)
B. \(14\)
C. \(15\)
D. \(13\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 12 greater than 217 is 228.

\[

\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 384 is the greatest multiple of 12 not exceeding 387.

\[
387=12\times 32+3
\]

\[
387-3=384
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 12.

\[
\frac{384-228}{12}+1=13+1=14
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 14.
Hence, there are 14 multiples of 12 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(14\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{387}{12}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{216}{12}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
32-18=14
\]

Fast result: \(14\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q283. Power \(2^{95}\) is tested modulo \(13\). Which remainder is obtained?
A. \(7\)
B. \(8\)
C. \(6\)
D. \(9\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
2\equiv 2\pmod{13}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 12.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 6\rightarrow 12\rightarrow 11\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 10\rightarrow 7\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(95-1)\bmod 12+1=11
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 7.

\[
2^{95}\equiv 7\pmod{13}
\]

Answer Verification
The 11th term of the cycle is 7.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(95-1)\bmod 12+1=11
\]

\[
2^{95}\equiv 7\pmod{13}
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q307. The number \(3x349\) is divisible by \(11\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(1\)
B. \(2\)
C. \(3\)
D. \(0\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the alternating-sum rule for 11

For divisibility by 11, the difference of alternate digit sums must be a multiple of 11.

\[
A=11
\]

\[
11-x\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Solve the alternating-sum equation

The valid multiple of 11 for this pattern is 11.

\[
11-x=11
\]

\[
x=0
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=0\), the alternate digit-sum difference is a multiple of 11.
\[
11-0=11
\]
\[
11\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(0\).

Shortcut

Write the alternate-sum expression and make it a multiple of 11.

\[
x\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

\[
x=0
\]

Fast result: \(0\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q309. For exponent \(122\), what ending digit does \(23^{122}\) have?
A. \(10\)
B. \(8\)
C. \(11\)
D. \(9\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
23\equiv 3\pmod{10}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 4.

\[
3\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 7\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(122-1)\bmod 4+1=2
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 9.

\[
23^{122}\equiv 9\pmod{10}
\]

Answer Verification
The 2nd term of the cycle is 9.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(9\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(122-1)\bmod 4+1=2
\]

\[
3^{122}\equiv 9\pmod{10}
\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[

\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 18.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(18\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |7-5|=18
\]

Fast result: \(18\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q313. Between \(158\) and \(327\), how many integers are divisible by \(9\)?
A. \(18\)
B. \(21\)
C. \(19\)
D. \(20\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 9 greater than 158 is 162.

\[
9\times 18=162
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 324 is the greatest multiple of 9 not exceeding 327.

\[
327=9\times 36+3
\]

\[
327-3=324
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 9.

\[
\frac{324-162}{9}+1=18+1=19
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 19.
Hence, there are 19 multiples of 9 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(19\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{327}{9}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{157}{9}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
36-17=19
\]

Fast result: \(19\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q320. The number \(892x4\) is divisible by \(12\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(3\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 12

A number divisible by 12 must be divisible by both 3 and 4.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 23.

\[
23+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+4=44
\]

\[
44\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 4.

\[
23+x=27
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=4\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
23+4=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
44\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 4\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+4\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q332. Between \(138\) and \(225\), how many integers are divisible by \(9\)?

B. \(12\)
C. \(10\)
D. \(11\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 9 greater than 138 is 144.

\[
9\times 16=144
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 225 is the greatest multiple of 9 not exceeding 225.

\[
225=9\times 25+0
\]

\[
225-0=225
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 9.

\[
\frac{225-144}{9}+1=9+1=10
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 10.
Hence, there are 10 multiples of 9 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(10\).

Shortcut

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{225}{9}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{137}{9}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
25-15=10
\]

Fast result: \(10\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q335. Power \(7^{25}\) is tested modulo \(9\). Which remainder is obtained?
A. \(7\)
B. \(8\)
C. \(6\)
D. \(9\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
7\equiv 7\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 3.

\[
7\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(25-1)\bmod 3+1=1
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 7.

\[
7^{25}\equiv 7\pmod{9}
\]

Answer Verification
The 1st term of the cycle is 7.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(25-1)\bmod 3+1=1
\]

\[
7^{25}\equiv 7\pmod{9}
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q359. The power \(7^{84}\) is very large. What are its final digit?
A. \(0\)
B. \(3\)
C. \(1\)
D. \(2\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
7\equiv 7\pmod{10}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 4.

\[
7\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(84-1)\bmod 4+1=4
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 1.

\[
7^{84}\equiv 1\pmod{10}
\]

Answer Verification
The 4th term of the cycle is 1.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(84-1)\bmod 4+1=4
\]

\[
7^{84}\equiv 1\pmod{10}
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Family: ns_cycle_length_detection
---

Q364. Digit sum \(14\) with tens digit \(6\) identifies which two-digit number?
A. \(69\)
B. \(67\)
C. \(70\)
D. \(68\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Form the digit-sum equation

\[
6+u=14\Rightarrow u=8
\]

Step 2: Write the two-digit number

Hence, the number is 68.

\[
N=10\times 6+8=68
\]

Answer Verification
Digit sum of 68 is 14.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(68\).

Shortcut

Find the ones digit from the digit sum, then place it.

\[
u=8
\]

\[
N=68
\]

Fast result: \(68\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q365. Between \(147\) and \(316\), how many integers are divisible by \(6\)?
A. \(27\)
B. \(30\)
C. \(28\)
D. \(29\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 6 greater than 147 is 150.

\[
6\times 25=150
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 312 is the greatest multiple of 6 not exceeding 316.

\[
316=6\times 52+4
\]

\[
316-4=312
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 6.

\[
\frac{312-150}{6}+1=27+1=28
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 28.
Hence, there are 28 multiples of 6 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(28\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{316}{6}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{146}{6}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
52-24=28
\]

Fast result: \(28\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q366. Bells ring at intervals of \(75\) and \(90\) minutes. After how many minutes will both ring together again?
A. \(495\)
B. \(427\)
C. \(450\)
D. \(473\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[
75=3\times 5^{2}
\]

\[
90=2\times 3^{2}\times 5
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(75,90)=450
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 450.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(450\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(75,90)=\frac{75\times 90}{\operatorname{HCF}(75,90)}=450
\]

Fast result: \(450\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q370. A factorial expression \(60!\) is given. What is the exponent of prime factor \(2\)?
A. \(56\)
B. \(57\)
C. \(55\)
D. \(58\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Count powers of 2 in n!

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{60}{2}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{60}{4}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{60}{8}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{60}{16}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{60}{32}\right\rfloor
\]

Step 2: Add the quotient terms

\[
30+15+7+3+1=56
\]

Answer Verification
Highest power of 2 dividing 60! is 56.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(56\).

Shortcut

Legendre sum of floor quotients only.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{60}{2}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{60}{4}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{60}{8}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{60}{16}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{60}{32}\right\rfloor
\]

Fast result: \(56\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q386. The number \(41x38\) is divisible by \(9\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(2\)
B. \(3\)
C. \(1\)
D. \(4\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the divisibility rule for 9

A number is divisible by 9 when the sum of its digits is a multiple of 9.

\[
S=16
\]

\[
S+x=16+x
\]

Step 2: Form and solve the digit-sum equation

The completed digit sum must be 18, a multiple of 9.

\[
16+x=18
\]

\[
x=2
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=2\), the digit-sum rule is satisfied.
\[
16+2=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(2\).

Shortcut

Complete the known digit sum to the next multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 2\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=2
\]

Fast result: \(2\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Family: ns_reverse_divisibility
---
Q387. Two buses leave a stand every \(84\) and \(156\) minutes. When will they next leave together?
A. \(983\)
B. \(1092\)
C. \(1201\)
D. \(1310\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[
84=2^{2}\times 3\times 7
\]

\[
156=2^{2}\times 3\times 13
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(84,156)=1092
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 1092.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(1092\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(84,156)=\frac{84\times 156}{\operatorname{HCF}(84,156)}=1092
\]

Fast result: \(1092\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q388. For the number \(12\), find the product of its positive divisors?
A. \(2074\)
B. \(1555\)
C. \(1728\)
D. \(1901\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
12=2^{2}\times 3
\]

Step 2: Count the divisors

\[
d(12)=(2+1)\times (1+1)=6
\]

Step 3: Use the product-of-divisors identity

\[
\prod d\mid 12 d=12^{d(12)/2}=12^{6/2}=1728
\]

Answer Verification
The product of all positive divisors is 1728.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(1728\).

Shortcut

After counting divisors, raise n to half the divisor count.

\[
12^{6/2}=1728
\]

Fast result: \(1728\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\[

\]

\[

\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(33-1)\bmod 2+1=1
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 6.

\[
13^{33}\equiv 6\pmod{7}
\]

Answer Verification
The 1st term of the cycle is 6.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(6\).

Shortcut

\[
p=(33-1)\bmod 2+1=1
\]

\[
6^{33}\equiv 6\pmod{7}
\]

Fast result: \(6\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\[

\]

\[

\]

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 6.

\[

\]

Fast result: \(27\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 25.

Final Answer

D. \(5\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
7\equiv 2\pmod{5}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 4.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(91-1)\bmod 4+1=3
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 3.

\[
7^{91}\equiv 3\pmod{5}
\]

Answer Verification
The 3rd term of the cycle is 3.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(3\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(91-1)\bmod 4+1=3
\]

\[
2^{91}\equiv 3\pmod{5}
\]

Fast result: \(3\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q439. The number \(49x16\) is divisible by \(9\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(7\)
B. \(8\)
C. \(6\)
D. \(9\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the divisibility rule for 9

A number is divisible by 9 when the sum of its digits is a multiple of 9.

\[
S=20
\]

\[
S+x=20+x
\]

Step 2: Form and solve the digit-sum equation

The completed digit sum must be 27, a multiple of 9.

\[
20+x=27
\]

\[
x=7
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=7\), the digit-sum rule is satisfied.
\[
20+7=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

Complete the known digit sum to the next multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 7\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=7
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q440. After prime factorising \(36\), what is the sum of all positive divisors?
A. \(92\)
B. \(90\)
C. \(93\)
D. \(91\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
36=2^{2}\times 3^{2}
\]

Step 2: Apply the sum-of-divisors rule

Multiply the geometric-sum factor for each prime power.

\[
\sigma(36)=91
\]

Answer Verification
The sum of all positive divisors is 91.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(91\).

Shortcut

Write prime powers once, then multiply the σ-factors.

\[
\frac{2^{3}-1}{2-1}\times \frac{3^{3}-1}{3-1}
\]

Fast result: \(91\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q445. Between \(144\) and \(281\), how many integers are divisible by \(9\)?
A. \(15\)
B. \(18\)
C. \(16\)
D. \(17\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 9 greater than 144 is 144.

\[
9\times 16=144
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 279 is the greatest multiple of 9 not exceeding 281.

\[
281=9\times 31+2
\]

\[
281-2=279
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 9.

\[
\frac{279-144}{9}+1=15+1=16
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 16.
Hence, there are 16 multiples of 9 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(16\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{281}{9}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{143}{9}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
31-15=16
\]

Fast result: \(16\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q449. The number \(5x096\) is divisible by \(11\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(3\)
B. \(1\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(2\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the alternating-sum rule for 11

For divisibility by 11, the difference of alternate digit sums must be a multiple of 11.

\[
A=2
\]

\[
2-x\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Solve the alternating-sum equation

The valid multiple of 11 for this pattern is 0.

\[
2-x=0
\]

\[
x=2
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=2\), the alternate digit-sum difference is a multiple of 11.
\[
2-2=0
\]
\[
0\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(2\).

Shortcut

Write the alternate-sum expression and make it a multiple of 11.

\[
x\equiv 2\pmod{11}
\]

\[
x=2
\]

Fast result: \(2\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

\]

Fast result: \(693\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q470. Between \(114\) and \(255\), how many integers are divisible by \(15\)?
A. \(9\)
B. \(12\)
C. \(10\)
D. \(11\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 15 greater than 114 is 120.

\[
15\times 8=120
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 255 is the greatest multiple of 15 not exceeding 255.

\[
255=15\times 17+0
\]

\[
255-0=255
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 15.

\[
\frac{255-120}{15}+1=9+1=10
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 10.
Hence, there are 10 multiples of 15 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(10\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{255}{15}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{113}{15}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
17-7=10
\]

Fast result: \(10\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q472. When \(11^{43}\) is divided by \(13\), what remainder is left?
A. \(1\)
B. \(4\)
C. \(2\)
D. \(3\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
11\equiv 11\pmod{13}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 12.

\[
11\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 7\rightarrow 12\rightarrow 2\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 10\rightarrow 6\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(43-1)\bmod 12+1=7
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 2.

\[
11^{43}\equiv 2\pmod{13}
\]

Answer Verification
The 7th term of the cycle is 2.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(2\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(43-1)\bmod 12+1=7
\]

\[
11^{43}\equiv 2\pmod{13}
\]

Fast result: \(2\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q479. The number \(3x094\) is divisible by \(9\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(2\)
B. \(3\)
C. \(1\)
D. \(4\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the divisibility rule for 9

A number is divisible by 9 when the sum of its digits is a multiple of 9.

\[
S=16
\]

\[
S+x=16+x
\]

Step 2: Form and solve the digit-sum equation

The completed digit sum must be 18, a multiple of 9.

\[
16+x=18
\]

\[
x=2
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=2\), the digit-sum rule is satisfied.
\[
16+2=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(2\).

Shortcut

Complete the known digit sum to the next multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 2\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=2
\]

Fast result: \(2\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q481. For the number \(20\), find the product of its positive divisors?
A. \(9600\)
B. \(7200\)
C. \(8000\)
D. \(8800\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
20=2^{2}\times 5
\]

Step 2: Count the divisors

\[
d(20)=(2+1)\times (1+1)=6
\]

Step 3: Use the product-of-divisors identity

\[
\prod d\mid 20 d=20^{d(20)/2}=20^{6/2}=8000
\]

Answer Verification
The product of all positive divisors is 8000.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(8000\).

Shortcut

After counting divisors, raise n to half the divisor count.

\[
20^{6/2}=8000
\]

Fast result: \(8000\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\]

Fast result: \(18\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q495. Between \(117\) and \(292\), how many integers are divisible by \(6\)?
A. \(28\)
B. \(31\)
C. \(29\)
D. \(30\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 6 greater than 117 is 120.

\[
6\times 20=120
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 288 is the greatest multiple of 6 not exceeding 292.

\[
292=6\times 48+4
\]

\[
292-4=288
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 6.

\[
\frac{288-120}{6}+1=28+1=29
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 29.
Hence, there are 29 multiples of 6 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(29\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{292}{6}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{116}{6}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
48-19=29
\]

Fast result: \(29\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q496. A number \(373\) is to be made divisible by \(9\). What is the minimum addition required?
A. \(5\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(7\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Combine the number conditions first

Optimization is done only after the divisibility or remainder condition is converted into one usable cycle.

Step 2: Move to the nearest valid boundary

The boundary step prevents picking a number that satisfies only the arithmetic but not the given range.

\[
373=9\times 41+4
\]

\[
9-4=5
\]

Answer Verification
The optimized valid value is 5.
\[
N=5
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Use the combined cycle and take only the needed boundary correction.

\[
Z=5
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q497. What is the least number greater than \(863\) that is divisible by both \(30\) and \(20\)?

C. \(855\)

Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Combine the number conditions first

Optimization is done only after the divisibility or remainder condition is converted into one usable cycle.

\[
m=60
\]

Step 2: Move to the nearest valid boundary

The boundary step prevents picking a number that satisfies only the arithmetic but not the given range.

\[
q=\left\lceil\frac{864}{60}\right\rceil
\]

\[
N=60\times q=900
\]

Answer Verification
The optimized valid value is 900.
\[
N=900
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(900\).

Shortcut

Fast result: \(900\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Family: ns_remainder_constraint_optimization

Q499. The number \(27x94\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(7\)
B. \(5\)
C. \(6\)
D. \(4\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 4, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
4\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 22; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
22+x=27
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=5\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
22+5=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
4\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 5\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Family: ns_reverse_divisibility
---
Q500. For the number \(4320\), find the sum of its positive divisors?
A. \(18144\)
B. \(13608\)
C. \(15120\)
D. \(16632\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
4320=2^{5}\times 3^{3}\times 5
\]

Step 2: Apply the sum-of-divisors rule

Multiply the geometric-sum factor for each prime power.

\[
\sigma(4320)=15120
\]

Answer Verification
The sum of all positive divisors is 15120.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(15120\).

Shortcut

Write prime powers once, then multiply the σ-factors.

\[
\frac{2^{6}-1}{2-1}\times \frac{3^{4}-1}{3-1}\times \frac{5^{2}-1}{5-1}
\]

Fast result: \(15120\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Family: ns_sum_of_divisors
